  14|                                                       SERVICE LEARNING
  15|                          The Monty Tech Marine Corps Junior Reserve Officer Training Corps (MCJROTC) is a leadership program
  16|                          designed to instill the values of citizenship, service, personal responsibility, and a sense of accomplishment
  17|                          into our young adults. The program provides students with countless opportunities to serve as ambassadors in
  18|                          their communities, through the routine demonstration of student leadership development, volunteerism,
  19|                          perseverance, and dedication.
  20|                          During the 2023 - 2024 school year, Monty Tech MCJROTC Cadets participated in more than 2,500 hours of
  21|                          community service and volunteerism. Cadets began the school year supporting three community 5K runs - the
  22|                          Fitchburg Veterans 5K, the Rock and Roll 5K at the Wachusett Brewery, and the Heroes Live Forever 5K also
  23|                          taking place at the Wachusett Brewery - providing assistance with vehicle parking, run route coordination, and
  24|                          vendor support. Cadets also enjoyed participating in the annual Blacksmith Festival held in Fitchburg, MA,
  25|                          where they assisted competitors and vendors, and while doing so, learned a great deal about community
  26|                          volunteerism and the craft of blacksmithing as a fine art.
  27|                           As in years past, Cadets were actively involved with the Fitchburg Salvation
  28|                           Army, and proudly assisted with the collection of donations that will go on
  29|                          to support the less fortunate throughout the community in a variety of ways.
  30|                          Cadets served as traditional bell ringers in front of many different
  31|                          community grocery and department stores, and conducted volunteer tolls
  32|                          throughout the region to support this effort.
  33|                           Assisting with color guards, presenting the Nation's Colors and the Marine
  34|                          Corps Battle Colors, is a particular honor and highlight for our young
  35|                          cadets. In 2023-2024, the Monty Tech Color Guard was able to support
  36|                          numerous events, including the Fitchburg State graduate and undergraduate
  37|                          ceremonies, local civic ceremonies, and numerous veteran ceremonies in
  38|                          and around the community especially during veteran holidays.

  12|                                                                                                                            In recent years, Monty Tech's
  13|                                                                                                                            popular School of Continuing
  14|                                       SCHOOL OF CONTINUING EDUCATION at                                                    Education, has been the recipient of
  15|                                       MONTACHUSETT REGIONAL VOCATIONAL TECHNICAL SCHOOL DISTRICT                           more than $2M in funding from

  19|                          cost training and job placement services in the region's most critical industries/occupations. These grant-
  20|                          funded training opportunities are an example of the collaboration between the MassHire Workforce Investment
  21|                          Board, the Executive Office of Labor and Workforce Development, and generous sponsorship from
  22|                          Commonwealth Corporation, making it possible for Monty Tech to offer meaningful job readiness training in
  23|                          occupations with high demand and self-sustaining wages.
  24|                          In 2023-2024, school leaders were pleased to offer 200-hour training programs in carpentry, culinary,
  25|                          electrical, plumbing, HVAC, CNC operation, welding and property maintenance, at no cost to qualified
  26|                          participants. These FY24 training cohorts resulted in 34 individuals having secured full-time employment in
  27|                          their chosen occupations. In addition to these grant-funded opportunities, the School of Continuing Education,
  28|                          continues to offer the area's most affordable and comprehensive cosmetology training program, effectively
  29|                          preparing participants to sit for the Massachusetts State Board examinations. In FY24, seven cosmetology
  30|                          students graduated from the program, and all have passed the licensing exam.
  31|                          Serving more than 1,700 community members annually, the School of Continuing Education is proud to offer
  32|                          meaningful training to a non-traditional student population, while working to develop new and important
  33|                          relationships with business partners in need of a talented candidate pool. The talented Continuing Education
  34|                          staff understands that the strength of any vocational training program is dependent upon mutually beneficial
  35|                          relationships between the school and our business partners, and they work tirelessly to build relationships
  36|                          throughout the year. One demonstration of such school-to-business collaboration is the school's annual job
  37|                          fair. In May of 2024, the School of Continuing Education hosted the annual event, welcoming more than
  38|                                                                                              131

===PAGE 132=== (ocr, 24 lines)
   1|                          seventy area employers who spent time visiting with both day and evening students seeking job opportunities.
   2|                          Numerous students attended the event, presented resumes to business representatives, and made contacts with
   3|                          employers that led to a first-time job placement and entry into the trades.
   4|                           The Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School Practical Nursing Program class of 2024
   5|                          achieved a 100% NCLEX-PN Pass Rate.
   6|                           We are thrilled to announce that 100% of our 2024 graduates passed the NCLEX-PN examination on their first
   7|                          attempt. This achievement reflects the dedication of our students, faculty, and staff to upholding the highest
   8|                          academic and clinical          standards as well as the program's dedication to preparing future nurses for the
   9|                          challenges of a career in healthcare.
  10|                          At the heart of this program's success lies a culture that prioritizes student success and professional readiness.
  11|                          Faculty members are not just educators but mentors, guiding students through a curriculum designed to build
  12|                          both theoretical knowledge and practical skills. The program emphasizes evidence-based learning, critical
  13|                          thinking, and compassionate care-key components of a well-rounded nursing education.
  14|                          The program's student-centered approach extends beyond academics. A robust support system ensures that
  15|                          students have access to resources that address their academic, emotional, and financial needs. Faculty advisors
  16|                          work closely with students to create tailored study plans as well as provide a safe space to navigate the stresses
  17|                          of nursing school.
  18|                          Looking forward we hope to increase program enrollment as well as community clinical partnerships to address
  19|                          the growing demand for skilled practical nurses.
  20|                          The program aims to maintain its 100% pass rate by continuously updating its curriculum, investing in faculty
  21|                          development, and expanding student resources. Leveraging technology and evidence-based teaching methods
  22|                          will remain a priority as we prepare the next generation of compassionate, skilled nurses who will make a
  23|                          meaningful impact on healthcare across North Central Massachusetts.
  24|                                                                                              132

===PAGE 133=== (ocr, 46 lines)
   1|                                                              LOOKING AHEAD
   2|                          Monty Tech pride is something students, alumni, family and friends demonstrate at a remarkable and humbling
   3|                          rate. Bumper stickers on countless vehicles, sweatshirts and hats worn to indicate pride in affiliation with the
   4|                          school, and shared stories of success heard in homes and across our communities are points of pride that do
   5|                          not go unnoticed to Monty Tech faculty and staff. The talented instructors, paraprofessionals, counselors,
   6|                          cafeteria staff, custodial and maintenance crew, and invaluable support staff work so hard every day to ensure
   7|                          all students have access to the best academic and vocational lessons, instructional technology and training
   8|                          equipment available, while learning in a safe and supportive environment. When we look ahead to what is
   9|                          next for Monty Tech, we begin with "more of the same" - excellence in education, building relationships with
  10|                          employers who rely on our workforce training programs, and working with the next generation of young
  11|                          leaders and entrepreneurs who make us so incredibly proud.
  12|                          In 2024-2025, Monty Tech leaders look forward to celebrating
  13|                          the opening of the school's first satellite campus, and home to
  14|                          MVP Academy.                Serving more than 80 students from
  15|                          partnering districts of Fitchburg, Gardner, and Narragansett
  16|                          Regional Schools, this new facility, made possible by a $1.9M
  17|                          Lab Modernization Grant (FY23), is Monty Tech's answer to
  18|                          the increasing number of students who seek a vocational high                                  MVP
  19|                          school experience, with no additional seats available. School
  20|                          leaders expect to expand even further the capacity at MVP                                     ACADEMY
  21|                           Academy, with the addition of a Plumbing training program
  22|                          by the end of FY26.
  23|                           Another deserving population that has had limited access to meaningful job training, are the area's young adults
  24|                          with disabilities. In 2023-2024, school leaders identified a grant opportunity and crafted a unique training program
  25|                          designed specifically for this population. That $133,900 award from Commonwealth Corporation enabled school
  26|                                                                                        leaders to roll out a small pilot program, training 12 deserving
  27|                                                                                        young adults in 4 different trades. Looking ahead and learning
  28|                                                                                        from our pilot program, school leaders are eager to apply for the
  29|                                                                                        grant again in FY25, so we may expand upon that successful
  30|                                                                                        program, doubling the number of students served and training
  31|                                                                                        programs offered. Program leaders also anticipate growing the
  32|                                                                                        number of employer partners, so that young adults with disabilities
  33|                                            Certiticate of Completion                   who enroll in the Supportive Training & Education Program
  34|                                                                                        (STEP) are matched with internships and job placements that align
  35|                                                                                        with their newfound skills and their special talents and abilities.
  36|                                                                                        Finally, FY25 will see two vocational program improvements that
  37|                                                                                        will undoubtedly please incoming and current students in the
  38|                                                                                        Culinary Arts and Carpentry programs.                   Thanks to continued
  39|                                                                                        partnerships with various grant agencies and donors, the school's
  40|                          Culinary Arts program will purchase $300,000 of new kitchen equipment and technology. Items that students will
  41|                          soon be trained on include a pasta extruder and various dyes, a gorgeous wood-fired brick pizza oven, new char
  42|                          broilers and skillets. Carpentry students will also enjoy new equipment, with the addition of two Advanced
  43|                          Construction Simulator Systems, that will teach students operator skills, using the same techniques found on
  44|                          worksites across the Commonwealth. Students will learn how to safely and effectively operate a variety of heavy
  45|                          equipment machinery, using these truly innovative experiential learning devices.
  46|                                                                                              133

  45|             gy Storage Systems" and by noting the manner in which it is permitted or prohibited in the various zoning dis-
  46|             tricts, as detailed below; and (ii) $250-4.13, which is a Section of the Protective Bylaw entitled "Solar energy
  47|             systems," by inserting a Subsection F entitled "Battery Energy Storage Systems" to reasonably regulate the

  49|             as detailed below; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board)
  50|                                                                                              148

===PAGE 149=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|             A. Proposed changes to §250-4.1G "Use Table"
   2|             A. Insert "Small Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems" into the Use Table and note that the use is
   3|                   permitted in all zoning districts.
   4|             B. Insert "Accessory Battery Energy Storage Systems" into the Use Table and note that the use is permitted
   5|                  in all zoning districts as an accessory use.
   6|             C. Insert "Medium-Scale Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems" and "Large-Scale Standalone Bat-
   7|                  tery Energy Storage Systems" into the Use Table and note that the uses are permitted as follows:
   8|                                   1. by right or by Site Plan Approval in the Solar Overlay District as provided.
   9|                                   2. by Site Plan Approval in the Commercial District and the Office Park/Industrial District.
  10|                                    3. by Special Permit from the Planning Board in the RA, RB, O, LB/R, R, VCD, SS, and W
  11|                                   districts.
  12|             D. Insert a footnote applicable to "Small Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems", "Accessory Battery
  13|             Energy Storage Systems", "Medium-Scale Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems", and "Large-Scale
  14|             Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems" indicating the uses are permitted as provided in §250-4.13F.
  15|             B. Proposed §250-4.13F
  16|             1. Purpose
  17|             The purpose of this subsection is to reasonably regulate the development and operation of Battery Energy
  18|             Storage Systems (BESS) by providing standards for the placement, design, construction, operation, monitor-
  19|             ing, modification, and removal of such systems that address public safety, minimize impacts on scenic, natu-
  20|             ral, and historic resources and to provide adequate financial assurance for the decommissioning of such sys-
  21|             tems in a manner which fulfills the following objectives:
  22|             A. To designate properties suitable for the location, construction and operation of BESS;
  23|             B. To ensure compatible land uses in the vicinity of the areas affected by BESS;
  24|             C. To mitigate the impacts of BESS on environmental resources, other protected resources, and private
  25|                  property; and
  26|             D. To create synergy between BESS development and the Commonwealth of Massachusetts Act to Ad-
  27|                   vance Clean Energy that established the Clean Peak Standard Energy Storage System.
  28|             2. Applicability
  29|             Provisions of this subsection §250-4.13F shall apply as follows:
  30|             A. To all Battery Energy Storage Systems as defined herein, which are permitted, installed, or modified in
  31|                  the Town of Lunenburg excluding general maintenance and repair.
  32|             B. To modifications, retrofits or replacements of an existing BESS that increase the total battery energy
  33|                   storage.
  34|             C. Except as an accessory to an approved solar generating facility, BESS facilities shall not be constructed
  35|                  or installed prior to the effective date of this by-law.
  36|             3. Definitions:
  37|             The definitions below are specific to the requirements of this subsection §250-4.13F and are intended to
  38|             supplement the standard definitions of the Zoning Bylaws, Article II (§250-2.1).
  39|             A. Battery Energy Storage Management System: A system that protects energy storage systems from operat-
  40|             ing outside their safe operating parameters and disconnects electrical power to the energy storage system or
  41|             places it in a safe condition if potentially hazardous temperatures or other conditions are detected.
  42|                                                                                              149

===PAGE 150=== (ocr, 49 lines)
   1|             B. Battery Energy Storage System: One or more devices, assembled together, capable of storing energy in or-
   2|                   der to supply electrical energy at a future time to the local power loads, to the utility grid, or for grid sup-
   3|                  port. For the purposes of this definition, a BESS shall not be considered a Public Utility and Facility.
   4|             C. Battery Energy Storage System, Accessory: A Battery Energy Storage System installed and operated to
   5|                   support an on-site Solar Generating Facility. This would include a Solar Parking Canopy as defined in
   6|                   §250-4.5A and §250-4.6A.
   7|             D. Battery Energy Storage System, Standalone: A system that is capable of absorbing energy from the electric
   8|                  grid, storing it for a period of time and thereafter distributing electricity.
   9|             E. Battery Energy Storage System, Small scale: A system which serves
  10|                    [1] a residential, commercial, industrial, or institutional development or
  11|                    [2] a parking facility which is not a solar generating facility but has one or more Electric Vehicle Charg-
  12|                    ing stations and the BESS is primarily associated with the Electric Vehicle Charging station functions.
  13|             F. Battery Energy Storage System, Medium scale: A pad site or sites no more than 10,000 square feet on a
  14|                  single parcel of land.
  15|             G. Battery Energy Storage System, Large scale: Pad site(s) larger than 10,000 square feet on a single parcel of
  16|                  land.
  17|             H. Pad compound: The enclosure (including the fence) in which one or more pads are installed upon which
  18|                  standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems are installed.
  19|             I. Pad site: Locations within a pad compound on which Battery Energy Storage Systems equipment are posi-
  20|                   tioned
  21|             J. Site Safety Plan: A plan which demonstrates the safe development, operation, and access to/from the facili-
  22|                   ty taking into consideration such factors as, but not limited to, means of access, roadway conditions, prox-
  23|                  imity to residential and other populated structures, hazardous materials, and monitoring protocols, etc.
  24|             K. Site Security Plan: A plan which details how the BESS will be secured from unauthorized entry and details
  25|                  the method of monitoring of entry.
  26|             4. Permitted Locations
  27|             A. Small BESS facilities may be located as part of the residential, commercial, industrial, or institutional de-
  28|                  velopment or parking facility it is serving provided it is included on the site plan or permit application ap-
  29|                  proved by the Town of Lunenburg for such facility.
  30|             B. BAccessory Battery Energy Storage Systems may be located as a part of any large-scale solar energy gen-
  31|                   erating facility permitted and developed in accordance with Section 250-4.13 of the Zoning Bylaw, provid-
  32|                   ed the BESS is depicted on the plans for which the solar energy generating facility is permitted.
  33|             C. Medium- or Large-scale Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems may be allowed by right within the
  34|                   Solar Overlay District provided they are to be located on parcels meeting the following minimum parcel
  35|                   sizes and spacing:
  36|                    [1] Medium-scale Standalone BESS installation: 6 acres and no less than 200 feet from residentially de-
  37|                    veloped property.
  38|                    [2] Large-scale Standalone BESS installation: 20 acres and no less than 200 feet from residentially devel
  39|                    oped property.
  40|             D. Medium- or Large-scale Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems may be permitted to be located with-
  41|                  in the Solar Overlay District by Site Plan Approval in accordance with section 250-8.4 of the Zoning By-
  42|                  law where the site does not conform to the size or spacing restrictions in Section 250-4.13F(4)(c) above.
  43|             E. E. Medium- or Large-scale Standalone Battery Energy Storage Systems may be allowed in the following
  44|                   [1] Commercial District
  45|                   [2] Office Park/Industrial District
  46|             F. Special Permit Required. Outside of the Solar Overlay District, Commercial District, and Office Park/
  47|                    Industrial District, medium- and large-scale Standalone Battery Storage Systems may be located on any
  48|                   lot containing more than 20 acres by special permit issued by the Planning Board.
  49|                                                                                              150

===PAGE 151=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|             5. Project Approval Criteria - Medium- and Large-Scale Standalone BESS projects
   2|             A. A. Standards - Site Plan Approval. In granting the Site Plan Approval, the Planning Board shall find that
   3|                  the permit may be granted without substantial detriment to the public good and without nullifying or sub-
   4|                  stantially derogating from the intent and purpose of this bylaw, and shall find that:
   5|                    [1] The project conforms to the standards for Site Plan Approval set forth in § 250-8.4E.
   6|                    [2] The granting of Site Plan Approval will not reasonably diminish the available light, air, sunlight, and
   7|                    other amenities.
   8|                    [3] There will be no nuisance or serious hazard to vehicles or pedestrians.
   9|                    [4] Environmental features of the site are protected, and surface runoff will not cause damage to sur-
  10|                    rounding properties or increase soil erosion and sedimentation of nearby streams and ponds.
  11|             B. Standards - Special Permit. In granting the Special Permit, the Planning Board shall find that the permit
  12|                  may be granted without substantial detriment to the public good and without nullifying or substantially
  13|                  derogating from the intent and purpose of this bylaw, and shall find that:
  14|                    [1] The project conforms to the standards for Site Plan Approval set forth in § 250-8.4E.
  15|                    [2] The specific site is an appropriate location for such an installation.
  16|                    [3] The proposed installation will not adversely affect the existing neighborhood by the following:
  17|                           [a] Historical significance,
  18|                                   [b] Scenic vistas,
  19|                                   [c] Natural and rural character, or
  20|                                   [d] Public safety.
  21|                                   [4] The granting of the Special Permit will not reasonably diminish the available light, air, sun
  22|                                   light, and other amenities.
  23|                                   [5] There will be no nuisance or serious hazard to vehicles or pedestrians.
  24|                                   [6] Environmental features of the site are protected, and surface runoff will not cause
  25|                                   damage to surrounding properties or increase soil erosion and sedimentation of
  26|                                   nearby streams and ponds.
  27|             C. The Planning Board may also impose conditions as it finds reasonably appropriate to safeguard the town or
  28|                  neighborhood including, but not limited to, screening, lighting, noise, fences, modification of the exterior
  29|                  appearance of electrical cabinets, battery storage systems, or other structures, limitation upon system size,
  30|                  and means of vehicular access or traffic features.
  31|             6. General Requirements - All BESS facilities
  32|             A. Building and Electrical Permits. Unless the Building Commissioner determines otherwise, a building per-
  33|                  mit and electrical permit are required prior to installation of the BESS facility.
  34|             B. Dimensional requirements. For purposes of dimensional requirements, facilities containing BESS equip-
  35|                  ment shall be treated as a building and subject to the more stringent requirements of the Zoning District in
  36|                  which it is located or this Section 250-4.13(F).
  37|             7. General Requirements - Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale BESS facilities
  38|             A. Containment. In the event an Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale BESS facility failure may result in dis-
  39|                  persal of chemicals, liquids, etc. which could be hazardous to the environment - particularly the ground
  40|                  water supply, BESS equipment storage/operations facilities shall be constructed with a means of properly
  41|                  containing such unplanned dispersals. Any such containment shall be provided, sized, and constructed as
  42|                  deemed appropriate and necessary by the Lunenburg Fire Chief or their designee.
  43|                                   [1] If the Fire Chief or their designee determines that the facility does not require a containment
  44|                                        structure, they shall state such in their Letter of Review of the Site Safety Plan which is to
  45|                                        be included the proponent's application to the Planning Board (Section 250-4.13F(13).
  46|             B. Security. A site security plan detailing all security measures and providing for an active security system
  47|                  monitored 24 hours per day shall be developed for each proposed Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale
  48|                                                                                              151

===PAGE 152=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|             BESS. Pursuant to 250-4.13F(15), this plan shall be submitted to and approved by the Lunenburg Police Chief
   2|             prior to submission of an application for project approval to the Planning Board. A copy of the Police Chief's
   3|             letter of approval must be included in the proponent's application.
   4|             C. Inspection. All medium and large-scale BESS facilities shall be open to inspection by the Fire Department,
   5|                  Police Department, and Building Commissioner during regular business hours with 24 hours' notice of re
   6|                  quest for such inspection to be made by the Town department or official. A property contact shall be availa-
   7|                  ble to such Town department or official 24 hours a day, seven days a week.
   8|             D. Maintenance and Operations
   9|                    [1] The owner/operator shall be responsible for maintenance of the facility including but not limited to the
  10|                     site, access, stormwater management system, structures, and security system. The owner/operator shall be
  11|                    solely responsible for the cost of maintaining the installation.
  12|                    [2] The applicant shall submit, as part of an application for Site Plan Approval and Special Permit for an
  13|                    Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale BESS, a maintenance and operations plan.
  14|             E. Hazardous Materials
  15|                    [1] Hazardous materials that are stored, used or generated on site shall not exceed the amount for a "Very
  16|                    Small Quantity Generator of Hazardous Waste" as defined by the Department of Environmental Protec-
  17|                    tion (DEP) pursuant to 310 CMR 30.000 and shall meet all requirements of the DEP and Massachusetts
  18|                    Contingency Plan, including storage of hazardous materials in a building with an impervious floor that is
  19|                    not adjacent to any floor drains to prevent discharge to the outdoor environment.
  20|             [A] If hazardous materials are utilized within the energy storage equipment, then impervious containment are-
  21|             as capable of controlling any release to the environment and to prevent potential contamination of groundwater
  22|             are required.
  23|             F. Safety Standards
  24|                    [1] At a minimum, all Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale BESS installations including equipment shall
  25|                    comply with the following:
  26|                                   [a] NFPA-855
  27|                                   [b] UL9540
  28|                                   [c] UL9540A
  29|                                   [d] UL1973
  30|                                   [e] 527 CMR 1 (Massachusetts Fire Code)
  31|             8. Design Standards - Accessory, Medium- and Large-Scale BESS
  32|             A. The design standards specified below shall be applicable to all Accessory, Medium-scale, and Large-scale
  33|             BESS projects.
  34|                    [1] Lighting and security. Lighting of applicable BESS facilities shall be sufficient for security and safe
  35|                    operational purposes and consistent with local, state and federal law. Lighting shall be reasonably shield-
  36|                    ed from abutting properties. Where feasible, any required lighting shall be directed downward and shall
  37|                    incorporate full-cut-off fixtures to reduce light pollution. Surveillance and security cameras shall be
  38|                    shielded from viewing abutting private property or invading the privacy of any abutting residential prop-
  39|                     erty owner.
  40|                    12] Signage. Signage shall be consistent with local, state, and tederal law. Signs shall be located and sized
  41|                    compliant with § 250-6.5 of the Zoning Bylaw and shall not be used for displaying any advertising except
  42|                    to identify the owner and/or operator of the solar installation and a twenty-four-hour emergency contact

   4|                    water management and as otherwise prescribed by applicable laws, regulations, and bylaws. In approving

   6|                    proximity to the proposed BESS equipment facilities if advised that such clearance is warranted by the

   8|             [4] Safety, emergency service and environmental standards. Design of the layout of the BESS facility shall be
   9|             consistent with the

  14|             as viewed from public roadways, and adjoining or nearby residential properties. Examples of methods of mini-
  15|             mizing such impacts include preserving natural vegetation to the maximum extent possible, using architectural
  16|             features, blending in equipment with the surroundings, incorporating earth berms, and adding vegetative buff-
  17|             ers to screen abutting residential properties, whether developed or not. Siting of installations shall be such that
  18|             the view of the installation from other areas of Town shall be as minimal as possible. Buffer strips shall sur-
  19|             round the proposed project.
  20|                                   [a] A screening plan, which assures the facility is shielded to greatest extent possible from pub-

  22|                                    ever is required

  26|             shall be surrounded by a buffer strip.

  28|                                   least 200 feet deep.

  30|                                   shall only be required to 50 feet deep.

  33|                                   buffering can be provided in a narrower buffer strip, the Planning Board may waive the afore-
  34|                                   mentioned depths and approve a narrower buffer strip,
  35|             [7] Height. The height of any structure (includes elements containing equipment) associated with an installa-

  38|                                   [a] Sufficient access for operation, maintenance, and emergency response must be provided and

  43|                                    Fire Codes and addressed as part of the Site Safety Plan review by the Lunenburg Fire Chief

   1|             [10] Noise. Noise generated by an Accessory, Medium-scale, or large-scale BESS installation, and by any as-

   3|             Department of Environmental Protection's Division of Air Quality noise regulations, 310 CMR 7.10.

   5|                                   perceived or impact the normal function of electronics off site.

  12|             shielded by a 7-foot-high fence.

===PAGE 155=== (ocr, 43 lines)
   1|             12. Abandonment, removal, and site restoration - Accessory, Medium-Scale, and Large-Scale BESS
   2|             A. Absent notice of the proposed date of decommissioning or written notice of extenuating circumstanc-
   3|                  es, the Battery Energy Storage System shall be considered abandoned when it fails to operate for
   4|                  more than one year without the written consent of the Planning Board.
   5|             B. The owner or operator shall physically remove the installation and stabilize/restore the site to the
   6|                  condition specified in the "restoration plan" approved by the Planning Board when the Planning
   7|                   Board approved the subject facility. This restoration plan shall be implemented no more than 150
   8|                  days after the date of abandonment or the proposed date of decommissioning operations, unless the
   9|                   Planning Board grants a waiver of this time requirement.
  10|             C. The owner or operator shall notify the Planning Board by certified mail of the proposed date of dis-
  11|                  continued operations and plans for removal and restoration. The notification shall consist of the
  12|                  methodology of physical removal of all structures, equipment, security barriers and transmission
  13|                  lines (located on the project site as part of the project operations), disposal of all solid and hazardous
  14|                   waste and stabilization or revegetation of the site.
  15|             [1] The Planning Board shall either approve or reject the plan at their next meeting.
  16|             [2] If the Planning Board rejects the plan, they shall do so only upon a finding that the plan fails to rea-
  17|             sonably remove the facility and restore the site to a condition usable for purposes allowed within the
  18|             zoning district in which the site is situated and shall state clear reasons for such rejection.
  19|             D. Landscaping, etc. may be left upon approval of the Planning Board.
  20|             E. If the owner or operator fails to remove the installation in accordance with the above criteria, the
  21|                   Town may, atter the receipt of an appropriate court order or consent of the property owner, enter the
  22|                   property and physically remove the installation at the owner's expense.
  23|             F. As a condition of a project permit, the project operator and property owner shall agree to allow the
  24|                   Town entry to remove an abandoned or decommissioned installation in the event it is not removed
  25|                   pursuant to notice given by the Town.
  26|             G. The cost for the removal will be charged to the property owner in accordance with the provisions of
  27|                   MGL c. 139, § 3A as a tax lien on the property.
  28|             13. Financial surety.
  29|             Proponents of medium and large-scale BESS projects shall provide a form of surety in a form acceptable
  30|             to the Town (generally, either through an escrow account, bond or similar method), to cover the cost of
  31|             removal and site restoration in the event the Town must remove the installation and remediate the land-
  32|             scape, in an amount and form determined to be reasonable by the Planning Board, but in no event to ex-
  33|             ceed more than 125% of the cost of removal and compliance with the additional requirements set forth
  34|             herein.
  35|             [1] The project proponent shall submit a fully inclusive estimate of the costs associated with removal and
  36|             site restoration, prepared by a qualified engineer.
  37|             [2] The Town's consulting engineer on the project review shall review and confirm or modify the esti-
  38|             mated costs and submit a recommendation to the Planning Board.
  39|             [3] The surety shall include a mechanism for calculating increased removal/restoration cost due to infla-
  40|             tion.
  41|             [4] Site restoration shall be for the purpose of restoring the site to a condition which allows reuse of the
  42|             site for a purpose permitted by right within the zoning district in which the site is located.

   2|             developed and submitted to the Fire Chief for approval prior to submission of an application for Planning

  25|                   demonstrate that there is no undue burden on Town public safety officials as a result of the proposed

  37|                   agencies.

  16|             ARTICLE 25. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Code of Lunenburg, to delete §250-4.9 Water Sup-
  17|             ply Protection District as written and replace by inserting $250-4.9 Water Supply Protection District as written
  18|             below; or take any other action relative thereto. (Submitted by the Planning Board)
  19|             250-4.9 WATER SUPPLY PROTECTION DISTRICT
  20|             A. PURPOSE OF DISTRICT
  21|             The purpose of this Water Supply Protection District is to:
  22|             1. promote the health, safety, and general welfare of the community by ensuring an adequate quality and
  23|                   quantity of drinking water for the Town of Lunenburg;
  24|             2. preserve and protect existing and potential sources of drinking water;
  25|             3. conserve natural resources in the Town of Lunenburg;
  26|             4. and prevent temporary and permanent contamination of the environment.
  27|             B. SCOPE OF AUTHORITY
  28|             1. The Water Supply Protection District is an overlay district superimposed on the zoning districts. This over-
  29|                  lay district shall apply to all new construction, reconstruction, or expansion of existing buildings and new
  30|                   or expanded uses. Applicable activities and uses in a portion of one of the underlying zoning districts that
  31|                  fall within the Water Supply Protection District must additionally comply with the requirements of this by-
  32|                  law. Uses prohibited in the underlying zoning districts shall not be permitted in the Water Supply Protec-
  33|                   tion District.
  34|             2. Nothing in 250-4.9 imposes regulation on parcels or portions of parcels located within Zone I.
  35|             C. DEFINITIONS
  36|             Automobile Graveyard: An establishment that is maintained, used, or operated for storing, keeping, buying, or
  37|             selling wrecked, scrapped, ruined, or motor vehicle parts as defined in MGL c.140B, s. 1.
  38|             Aquifer: A geologic formation composed of rock, sand or gravel that contains significant amounts of potential-
  39|             ly recoverable water.
  40|             CMR: Code of Massachusetts Regulations.
  41|                                                                                              157

===PAGE 158=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|             Commercial Fertilizer: Any substance containing one or more recognized plant nutrients which is used for its
   2|             plant nutrient content and which is designed for use, or claimed to have value in promoting plant growth, ex-
   3|             cept un-manipulated animal and vegetable manures, marl, lime, limestone, wood ashes, and gypsum, and other
   4|             products exempted by state regulations.
   5|             Discharge: The accidental or intentional disposal, deposit, injection, dumping, spilling, leaking, pouring, or
   6|             placing of toxic or hazardous material or hazardous waste upon or into any land or water such that it may enter
   7|             the surface or ground waters.
   8|             Dry Well: A subsurface pit with open-jointed lining or holes through which storm- water drainage from roofs,
   9|             basement floors, foundations or other areas seep into the surrounding soil.
  10|             Water Supply Protection District: The land area consisting of aquifers, Zone II recharge areas, and Zone III
  11|             areas, all ass approved by MassDEP and identified on a map and adopted pursuant to this bylaw.
  12|             Hazardous Material: Any substance in any form which because of its quantity, concentration, or its chemical,
  13|             corrosive, flammable, reactive, toxic, infectious or radioactive characteristics, either separately or in combina-
  14|             tion with one or more substances, constitutes a present or potential threat to human health, safety, welfare, or
  15|             to the environment, when improperly stored, treated, transported, disposed of, used, or otherwise managed.
  16|             Hazardous material includes, without limitation, synthetic organic chemicals, petroleum products, heavy met-
  17|             als, radioactive or infectious materials, and all substances defined as toxic or hazardous under MGL c. 21E.
  18|             This term shall not include hazardous waste or oil.
  19|             Historical High Groundwater Table Elevation: A groundwater elevation determined from monitoring wells and
  20|             historical water table fluctuation data compiled by the United States Geological Survey.
  21|             Hazardous Waste: A substance or combination of substances, which because of quantity, concentration, or
  22|             physical, chemical or infectious characteristics may cause, or significantly contribute to an increase in mortali-
  23|             ty or an increase in serious irreversible, or incapacitating reversible illness or pose a substantial present or po-
  24|             tential hazard to human health, safety, or welfare or to the environment when improperly treated, stored, trans-
  25|             ported, used or disposed of, or otherwise managed.
  26|             This term shall include all substances identified as hazardous pursuant to the Hazardous Waste Regulations,
  27|             310 CMR 30.000.
  28|             Impervious Surface: Material or structure on, above, or below the ground that does not allow precipitation or
  29|             surface water runoff to penetrate into the soil.
  30|             Interim Wellhead Protection Area (IWPA): The MassDEP designated protection radius around a public water
  31|             well that lacks a Zone II.
  32|                                                                                              158

===PAGE 159=== (ocr, 30 lines)
   1|              Junkyard: An establishment that is maintained, operated, or used for storing, keeping, buying, or selling junk,
   2|              or for the maintenance or operation of an automobile graveyard, as defined in MGL c.140B, s.1.
   3|              Landfill: A facility established in accordance with a valid site assignment for the purposes of disposing solid
   4|              waste into or on the land, pursuant to the Solid Waste Regulations, 310 CMR 19.006.
   5|              MassDEP: Massachusetts Department of Environmental Protection. MGL: Massachusetts General Law.
   6|              Petroleum Product: Includes, but not limited to, fuel oil; gasoline; diesel; kerosene; aviation jet fuel; aviation
   7|              gasoline; lubricating oils; oily sludge; oil refuse; oil mixed with other wastes; crude oils; or other liquid hy-
   8|              drocarbons regardless of specific gravity. Petroleum product shall not include liquefied petroleum gas includ-
   9|              ing, but not limited to, liquefied natural gas, propane or butane.
  10|              Non-Sanitary Wastewater: Wastewater discharges from industrial and commercial facilities containing wastes
  11|              from any activity other than collection of sanitary sewage including, but not limited to, activities specified in
  12|              310 CMR 15.004(6).
  13|              Open Dump: A facility operated or maintained in violation of the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act
  14|              42 U.S.C. 4004(a)(b), or state regulations and criteria for solid waste disposal.
  15|              Recharge Areas: Land areas, such as a Zone II or an Interim Wellhead Protection Area, where precipitation
  16|              and surface water infiltrates into the ground to replenish groundwater and aquifers used for public drinking
  17|              water supplies.
  18|              Septage: The liquid, solid, and semi-solid contents of privies, chemical toilets, cesspools, holding tanks, or
  19|              other sewage waste receptacles. This term shall not include any material that is a hazardous waste, as defined
  20|              by 310 CMR 30.000.
  21|              Sludge: The solid, semi-solid, and liquid residue that results from a process of wastewater treatment or drink-
  22|              ing water treatment including wastewater residuals. This term shall not include grit, screening, or grease and
  23|              oil which are removed at the headworks of a facility
  24|              Treatment Works: Any and all devices, processes and properties, real or personal, used in the collection,
  25|              pumping, transmission, storage, treatment, disposal, recycling, reclamation, or reuse of waterborne pollutants,
  26|              but not including any works receiving a hazardous waste from off the site of the works for the purpose of
  27|               treatment, storage, or disposal.
  28|              Utility Works: Regulated activities providing for public services, including roads, water, sewer, electricity,
  29|              gas, telephone, transportation and their associated maintenance activities. This term shall include the install-
  30|                                                                                              159

===PAGE 160=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|             -ation of detention and retention basins for the purpose of controlling storm water.
   2|             Very Small Quantity Generator: Any public or private entity, other than residential, which produces less than
   3|             27 gallons (100 kilograms) a month of hazardous waste or waste oil, but not including any acutely hazardous
   4|             waste as defined in 310 CMR 30.136.
   5|             Waste Oil Retention Facility: A waste oil collection facility for automobile service stations, retail outlets, and
   6|             marinas which is sheltered and has adequate protection to contain a spill, seepage, or discharge of petroleum
   7|             waste products in accordance with MGL c.21. s.52A.
   8|             Zone I: A protective radius around a public water supply well or wellfield as approved by MassDEP and de-
   9|             fined under the Massachusetts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00. No parcels or portions of parcels
  10|             within Zone I shall be subject to any regulations imposed by 250-4.9.
  11|             Zone II: The delineated recharge area to a public drinking water well as approved by MassDEP and defined
  12|             under the Massachusetts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00. For the purposes of this § 250-4.9,
  13|             Zone II shall not extend within that area included in Zone I. Accordingly, no parcels or portions of parcels lo-
  14|             cated within Zone I are considered to be included within Zone II.
  15|             Zone III: An area beyond a Zone II recharge area, as approved by MassDEP and defined under the Massachu-
  16|             setts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00.
  17|             D. ESTABLISHMENT AND DELINEATION OF WATER SUPPLY PROTECTION DISTRICT
  18|             For the purposes of this bylaw, there are hereby established within the Town of Lunenburg certain water sup-
  19|             ply protection areas a Zone I, Zone II, and Zone III which consist of a protective radius around a public water
  20|             supply well or wellfield (Zone I), aquifers or recharge areas (Zone II, and certain protected areas beyond those
  21|             Zone II areas (Zone III). These areas are delineated on a map entitled Lunenburg Water District Aquifer Pro-
  22|             tection Zones dated July 2021 which is hereby made part of the Water Supply Protection District Bylaw and is
  23|             on file in the office of the Town Clerk.
  24|             E. DISTRICT BOUNDARY DISPUTES
  25|             1. If the location of the Water Supply Protection District in relation to a particular parcel is in doubt, resolu-
  26|                   tion of the boundary dispute shall be through a Special Permit application to the Special Permit Granting
  27|                   Authority. Any application for a special permit for this purpose shall be accompanied by adequate docu-
  28|                   mentation.
  29|             2. Burden of proof shall be upon the landowner to demonstrate that the location of the Water Supply Protec-
  30|                   tion District with respect to a particular parcel(s) of land is uncertain. At the request of the landowner, the
  31|                   Town may engage a professional engineer, hydrologist, geologist, or soil scientist to determine more accu-
  32|                  rately the boundaries of the Water Supply Protection District with respect to a particular parcel(s) of land,
  33|                  and may charge the owner for the cost of the investigation.
  34|                   A. Changes to the Water Supply Protection District require town meeting approval.
  35|                   B. Changes to the Zone II boundary require approval from MassDEP.
  36|                                                                                              160

===PAGE 161=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|             3. Where the Zone II boundary line of the Water Supply Protection District divides a lot or parcel, the re-
   2|                  quirements established by this bylaw shall apply to the land 20 feet beyond the Zone II boundary.
   3|             F. PERMITTED USES
   4|             1. The following uses are permitted within Zone II and Zone III of the Water Supply Protection District, pro-
   5|             vided that all necessary permits, orders, or approvals required by local, state, or federal law are also obtained:
   6|             A. conservation of soil, water, plants, and wildlife;
   7|             B. outdoor recreation, nature study, boating, fishing, and hunting where otherwise legally permitted;
   8|             C. foot, bicycle and/or horse paths, and bridges;
   9|             D. normal operation and maintenance of existing water bodies and dams, splash boards, and other water con-
  10|                   trol, supply and conservation devices;
  11|             E. maintenance, repair, and enlargement of any existing structure, subject to § 250-4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and §
  12|                  250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw;
  13|             F. residential development, subject to § 250-4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw;
  14|             G. farming, gardening, nursery, conservation, forestry, harvesting, and grazing, subject to § 250-4.9 G, § 250-
  15|                  4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw;
  16|             H. construction, maintenance, repair, and enlargement of drinking water supply related facilities such as, but
  17|                  not limited to, wells, pipelines, aqueducts, and tunnels; and
  18|             I. any use permitted in the underlying zoning by-right or by special permit, except for those uses specifically
  19|                  prohibited in § 250-4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw.
  20|             G. PROHIBITED USES
  21|             1. The following land uses and activities are prohibited in Zone II unless designed in accordance with the spec-
  22|             ified performance standards :
  23|             A. landfills and open dumps;
  24|             B. automobile graveyards and junkyards,;
  25|             C. landfills receiving only wastewater residuals and/or septage, including those approved by MassDEP pursu-
  26|                  ant to MGL c. 21 s.26 through s.53, MGL c.111 s.17, and MGL c.83 s.6 and s.7;
  27|             D. facilities that generate, treat, store, or dispose of hazardous waste that are subject to MGL c.21C and 310
  28|                   CMR 30.000, except for:
  29|             1. very small quantity generators as defined under 310 CMR 30.000;
  30|             2. household hazardous waste centers and events under 310 CMR 30.390;
  31|             3. waste oil retention facilities required by MGL c. 21, s.52A; and
  32|             4. water remediation treatment works approved by MassDEP for the treatment of contaminated waters.
  33|             E. petroleum, fuel oil, and heating oil bulk stations and terminals including, but not limited to, those listed un-
  34|             der North American Industry Classification System (NAICS) Codes 424710 and 454311, except for liquefied
  35|             petroleum gas.
  36|                                                                                              161

===PAGE 162=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|             F. storage of liquid hazardous materials and/or liquid petroleum products unless such storage is above ground-
   2|                        level and on an impervious surface and either:
   3|             1. in container(s) or above ground tank(s) within a building; or
   4|             2. outdoors in covered container(s) or above ground tank(s) in an area that has a containment system designed
   5|                  and operated to hold either; 10% of the total possible storage capacity of all containers or 110% of the larg-
   6|                   est container's storage capacity, whichever is greater.
   7|             however, these storage requirements shall not apply to the replacement of existing tanks or systems for the
   8|                        keeping, dispensing or storing of gasoline provided the replacement is performed in a manner con-
   9|                        sistent with state and local requirements;
  10|             G. storage of sludge and septage, unless such storage is in compliance with 310 CMR 32.30 and 310 CMR
  11|                  32.31;
  12|             H. storage of deicing chemicals, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and ready for ap-
  13|                   plication, unless such storage, is within a structure designed to prevent the generation and escape of con-
  14|                  taminated runoff or leachate;
  15|             I.  storage of animal manure, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and ready for applica-
  16|                  tion, unless contained within a structure designed to prevent the generation and escape of contaminated
  17|                   runoff or leachate;
  18|             J. storage of commercial fertilizers, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and ready for
  19|                   application, unless such storage is within a structure designed to prevent the generation and escape of con-
  20|                  taminated runoff or leachate;
  21|             K. stockpiling and disposal of snow and ice containing deicing chemicals brought in from outside the Water
  22|                   Supply Protection District;
  23|             L. earth removal, consisting of the removal of soil, loam, sand, gravel, or any other earth material to within 4
  24|                  feet of historical high groundwater as determined from monitoring wells and historical water table fluctua-
  25|                   tion data compiled by the United States Geological Survey, except for excavations for building founda-
  26|                  tions, roads, utility works or wetland restoration work conducted in accordance with a valid Order of Con-
  27|                   dition issued pursuant to MGL c. 131, s.40; and
  28|             M. treatment or disposal works subject to 314 CMR 5.00, for non- sanitary wastewater, including those activi-
  29|                   ties listed under 310 CMR 15.004(6), except for:
  30|             1. treatment works approved by MassDEP designed for the treatment of contaminated ground or surface wa-
  31|                  ter and operating in compliance with 314 CMR 5.05(3) or 5.05(13); and
  32|             2. publicly owned treatment works.
  33|             H. USES AND ACTIVITIES REQUIRING A SPECIAL PERMIT
  34|             1. In Zone II the following uses and activities are permitted only upon the issuance of a Special Permit by the
  35|                        Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) under such conditions as they may require:
  36|             A. enlargement or alteration of existing uses that do not conform to the Water Supply Protection District;
  37|             B. except as prohibited under § 250-4.9 G of this bylaw, activities that involve the handling of toxic or haz-
  38|                  ardous materials in quantities greater than those associated with normal household use and which are per-
  39|                   mitted in the underlying zoning district;
  40|             C. rendering impervious any lot or parcel more than 15% or 2,500 square feet, whichever is greater; unless
  41|                  artificial recharge, that will not degrade water quality, is provided using methods demonstrated to be capa-
  42|                  ble of removing contaminants from storm water and which are consistent with methods described in
  43|                   MassDEP's Stormwater Handbook, Vol. I, II and III, as amended.
  44|             I. PROCEDURES FOR ISSUANCE OF SPECIAL PERMIT
  45|             1. The Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) under this bylaw shall be the Planning Board. A special per-
  46|             mit shall be granted if the SPGA determines, with advisement from the Lunenburg Water District, Building
  47|             Commissioner, Conservation
  48|                                                                                              162

===PAGE 163=== (ocr, 51 lines)
   1|             Commission, Department of Public Works, and Board of Health, that the intent of this bylaw, as well as its
   2|             specific criteria, are met. The SPGA shall not grant a special permit under this section unless the petitioner's
   3|             application materials include, in the SPA's opinion, sufficiently detailed, definite, and credible information to
   4|             support positive findings in relation to the standards given in this section. The SPGA shall document the basis
   5|             for any departures from the recommendations of the other municipal boards, departments or commissions in its
   6|             decision.
   7|             2. Upon receipt of the special permit application, the SPGA shall transmit one copy to the Lunenburg Water
   8|                   District, Building Commissioner, Conservation Commission, Department of Public Works, Board of
   9|                   Health, and Lunenburg Fire Department. Failure to respond in writing within 35 days of receipt shall be
  10|                  considered as no desire to comment. The necessary number of copies of the application shall be furnished
  11|                   by the applicant.
  12|             3. The SPGA may grant the required special permit upon finding that the proposed use meets the following
  13|                   standards, those standards and requirements specified in § 250-4.9 G of this bylaw, and any regulations or
  14|                   guidelines adopted by the SPGA. The proposed use must:
  15|             B. in no way, during construction or thereafter, materially adversely affect the quality or quantity of the water
  16|                  supplies protected by the Water Supply Protection District; and
  17|             C. be designed to avoid substantial disturbance of the soils, topography, drainage, vegetation, and other water-
  18|                  related natural characteristics of the site to be developed, except as depicted on an approved site plan.
  19|             4. The SPGA may adopt controls to govern design features of projects. Such controls shall be consistent with
  20|                  the Town's subdivision regulations, Site Plan Approval, and Stormwater Bylaws.
  21|             5. The applicant shall file 10 copies of a site plan and attachments. The site plan shall be drawn at a proper
  22|                  scale as determined by the SPGA and be stamped by a professional engineer. All additional submittals
  23|                   shall be prepared by qualified professionals. The site plan and all supporting materials must meet the re-
  24|                   quirements outlined in the Planning Board Rules & Regulations for Site Plan Approval and Special Permit
  25|                  and at a minimum include the following information where pertinent:
  26|             A. a complete list of the categories of chemicals, pesticides, herbicides, fertilizers, fuels, and other potentially
  27|                  hazardous materials to be used or stored on the premises in quantities greater than those associated with
  28|                  normal household use; and
  29|             B. for activities using or storing hazardous materials or wastes, a management plan shall be prepared and filed
  30|                   with the Fire Chief and the Board of Health. The plan will be consistent with the standards and require-
  31|                  ments set forth in § 250-4.9 G above and shall include:
  32|                              1. provisions to protect against the discharge of hazardous materials or wastes to the environment
  33|                                   due to spillage, accidental damage, corrosion, leakage, or vandalism, including spill contain-
  34|                                   ment and clean-up procedures;
  35|                              2. provisions for indoor, secured storage of hazardous materials or wastes with impervious floor
  36|                                   surfaces;
  37|                              3. evidence of compliance with the Massachusetts Hazardous Waste Regulations 310 CMR
  38|                                   30.000; and
  39|                              4. proposed down-gradient location(s) for groundwater monitoring wells), should the SPGA
  40|                                    deem the activity a potential groundwater threat
  41|             6. The SPGA shall hold a hearing, in contormity with the provision of MGL C.40A s. 9, within 65 days atter
  42|                  the filing of the application. Notice of the public hearing shall be given by publication and posting and by
  43|                  first-class mailings to "parties of interest" as defined in MGL c.40A s.11. The decision of the SPGA and
  44|                  any extension, modification, or renewal thereof shall be filed with the SPGA and Town Clerk within 90
  45|                   days following the closing of the public hearing. Failure of the SPGA to act within 90 days shall be
  46|                   deemed as a granting of the permit.
  47|             7. If there are material changes to the categories of potentially hazardous materials to be used or stored on the
  48|                  premises in quantities greater than those associated with normal household use after the Special Permit has
  49|                   been grantea, the owner/operator will file updated information on those categories of hazardous materials
  50|                   with the Fire Department, the Board of Health, and the Planning Board.
  51|                                                                                              163

  22|             K. ENFORCEMENT
  23|             1. Written notice of any violations of this bylaw shall be given by the Building Commissioner to the responsi-
  24|                   ble person as soon as possible after detection of a violation or a continuing violation. Notice to the assessed
  25|                  owner of the property shall be deemed notice to the responsible person. Such notice shall specify the re-
  26|                  quirement or restriction violated and the nature of the violation, and may also identify the actions necessary
  27|                  to remove or remedy the violations and preventive measures required for avoiding future violations and a
  28|                  schedule of compliance.
  29|             2. A copy of such notice shall be submitted to the Land Use Director, Building Commissioner, Conservation
  30|                   Commission, Department of Public Works, and Board of Health. The cost of containment, clean-up, or
  31|                  other action of compliance shall be borne by the owner/operator of the premises.
  32|             3. The Building Commissioner may use any portion of Section 250-8.2 of these Zoning Bylaws to assist in
  33|                   the entorcement of the requirements of this Bylaw.
