   5|             ply Protection District, as written below (Zoning Map is the last page in the Voter's Guide booklet; VOTED
   6|             w/amendment in italic to Sections C and D (Yes 195, No 16 Abstain 0 Total 211) (Planning Board & Se-
   7|             lect Board recommend Approval; Finance Committee No Financial Impact) to amend the Code of
   8|             Lunenburg by deleting Section 250-4.9, entitled Water Supply Protection District, in its entirety and in-
   9|             serting a new Section 250-4.9, entitled Water Supply Protection District as follows: (The Zoning Map
  10|             was a handout at the meeting - not the last page of the Voters Guide Booklet.)
  11|             250-4.9 WATER SUPPLY PROTECTION DISTRICT
  12|             A. PURPOSE OF DISTRICT
  13|             The purpose of this Water Supply Protection District is to:
  14|             1. Promote the health, safety, and general welfare of the community by ensuring an adequate quality and
  15|                   quantity of drinking water for the Town of Lunenburg;
  16|             2. preserve and protect existing and potential sources of drinking water;
  17|             3. Conserve natural resources in the Town of Lunenburg; and
  18|             4. Prevent temporary and permanent contamination of the environment.
  19|             B. SCOPE OF AUTHORITY
  20|             1. The Water Supply Protection District is an overlay district superimposed on the zoning districts. This over-
  21|                  lay district shall apply to all new construction, reconstruction, or expansion of existing buildings and new
  22|                  or expanded uses. Applicable activities and uses in a portion of one of the underlying zoning districts that
  23|                  fall within the Water Supply Protection District must additionally comply with the requirements of this by-
  24|                  law. Uses prohibited in the underlying zoning districts shall not be permitted in the Water Supply Protec-
  25|                   tion District.
  26|             C. DEFINITIONS
  27|             Automobile Graveyard: An establishment that is maintained, used, or operated for storing, keeping, buying, or
  28|             selling wrecked, scrapped, ruined, or motor vehicle parts as defined in MGL c.140B, s.1.
  29|             Aquifer: A geologic formation composed of rock, sand or gravel that contains significant amounts of potential-
  30|             ly recoverable water.
  31|             CMR: Code of Massachusetts Regulations.
  32|             Commercial Fertilizer: Any substance containing one or more recognized plant nutrients which is used for its
  33|             plant nutrient content and which is designed for use, or claimed to have value in promoting plant growth, ex-
  34|             cept un-manipulated animal and vegetable manures, marl, lime, limestone, wood ashes, and gypsum, and other
  35|             products exempted by state regulations.
  36|             Contaminated Groundwater: Groundwater containing oil and/or hazardous material at concentrations equal to
  37|             or greater than a release notification threshold established by 310 CMR 40.0300 and 40.1600.
  38|             Contaminated Surface Water: Surface water containing oil and/or hazardous material associated with a release
  39|             for which notification is required under 310 CMR 40.0300 and 40.1600.
  40|             Discharge: The accidental or intentional disposal, deposit, injection, dumping, spilling, leaking, pouring, or
  41|             placing of toxic or hazardous material or hazardous waste upon or into any land or water such that it may enter
  42|             the surface or ground waters.
  43|                                                                                              166

===PAGE 167=== (ocr, 40 lines)
   1|             Dry Well: A subsurface pit with open-jointed lining or holes through which storm- water drainage from roofs,
   2|             basement floors, foundations, or other areas seep into the surrounding soil.
   3|             Water Supply Protection District: The land area consisting of aquifers, Zone II recharge areas, and Zone III
   4|             areas, all as approved by MassDEP and identified on a map and adopted pursuant to this bylaw.
   5|             Hazardous Material: Any substance in any form which because of its quantity, concentration, or its chemical,
   6|             corrosive, flammable, reactive, toxic, infectious or radioactive characteristics, either separately or in combina-
   7|             tion with one or more substances, constitutes a present or potential threat to human health, safety, welfare, or
   8|             to the environment, when improperly stored, treated, transported, disposed of, used, or otherwise managed.
   9|             Hazardous material includes, without limitation, synthetic organic chemicals, petroleum products, heavy met-
  10|             als, radioactive or infectious materials, and all substances defined as toxic or hazardous under MGL C. 21E. This term shall not include hazardous waste or oil.
  11|             Historical High Groundwater Table Elevation: A groundwater elevation determined from monitoring wells and
  12|             historical water table fluctuation data compiled by the United States Geological Survey.
  13|             Hazardous Waste: A substance or combination of substances, which because of quantity, concentration, or
  14|             hor al, chrase i sides is charace, or sic pay a reversenie ilms ortise a so a antial present or ali-
  15|             tential hazard to human health, safety, or welfare or to the environment when improperly treated, stored, trans-
  16|             ported, used or disposed of, or otherwise managed. This term shall include all substances identified as hazard-
  17|             ous pursuant to the Hazardous Waste Regulations, 310 CMR 30.000.
  18|             Impervious Surface: Material or structure on, above, or below the ground that does not allow precipitation or
  19|             surface water runoff to penetrate into the soil.
  20|             Interim Wellhead Protection Area (IWPA): The MassDEP designated protection radius around a public water
  21|             well that lacks a Zone II.
  22|             Junkyard: An establishment that is maintained, operated, or used for storing, keeping, buying, or selling junk,
  23|             or for the maintenance or operation of an automobile graveyard, as defined in MGL c.140B, s.1.
  24|             Landfill: A facility established in accordance with a valid site assignment for the purposes of disposing solid
  25|             waste into or on the land, pursuant to the Solid Waste
  26|             Regulations, 310 CMR 19.006.
  27|             MassDEP: Massachusetts Department of Environmental Protection. MGL: Massachusetts General Law.
  28|             Petroleum Product: Includes, but not limited to, fuel oil; gasoline; diesel; kerosene; aviation jet fuel; aviation
  29|             gasoline; lubricating oils; oily sludge; oil refuse; oil mixed with other wastes; crude oils; or other liquid hydro-
  30|             carbons regardless of specific gravity. Petroleum product shall not include liquefied petroleum gas including,
  31|             but not limited to, liquefied natural gas, propane, or butane.
  32|             Non-Sanitary Wastewater: Wastewater discharges from industrial and commercial facilities containing wastes
  33|             from any activity other than collection of sanitary sewage including, but not limited to, activities specified in
  34|             310 CMR 15.004(6).
  35|             Open Dump: A facility operated or maintained in violation of the Resource Conservation and Recovery Act 42
  36|             U.S.C. 4004(a)(b), or state regulations and criteria for solid waste disposal.
  37|             Recharge Areas: Land areas, such as a Zone II or an Interim Wellhead Protection Area, where precipitation
  38|             and surface water infiltrates into the ground to replenish groundwater and aquifers used for public drinking
  39|             water supplies.
  40|                                                                                              167

===PAGE 168=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|             Septage: The liquid, solid, and semi-solid contents of privies, chemical toilets, cesspools, holding tanks, or
   2|             other sewage waste receptacles. This term shall not include any material that is a hazardous waste, as defined
   3|             by 310 CMR 30.000.
   4|             Sludge: The solid, semi-solid, and liquid residue that results from a process of wastewater treatment or drink-
   5|             ing water treatment including wastewater residuals. This term shall not include grit, screening, or grease and
   6|             oil which are removed at the headworks of a facility.
   7|             Treatment Works: Any and all devices, processes and properties, real or personal, used in the collection,
   8|             pumping, transmission, storage, treatment, disposal, recycling, reclamation, or reuse of waterborne pollutants,
   9|             but not including any works receiving a hazardous waste from off the site of the works for the purpose of treat-
  10|             ment, storage, or disposal.
  11|             Utility Works: Regulated activities providing for public services, including roads, water, sewer, electricity,
  12|             gas, telephone, transportation, and their associated maintenance activities. This term shall include the installa-
  13|             tion of detention and retention basins for the purpose of controlling storm water.
  14|             Very Small Quantity Generator: Any public or private entity, other than residential, which produces less than
  15|             27 gallons (100 kilograms) a month of hazardous waste or waste oil, but not including any acutely hazardous
  16|             Waste Oil Retention Facility: A waste oil collection facility for automobile service stations, retail outlets, and
  17|             marinas which is sheltered and has adequate protection to contain a spill, seepage, or discharge of petroleum
  18|             waste products in accordance with MGL c.21. s.52A.
  19|             Zone I: A protective radius around a public water supply well or wellfield as approved by MassDEP and de-
  20|             fined under the Massachusetts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00. "provided, however, that Zone 1
  21|             ends at the water's edge and does not extend into the waters of Hickory Hills Lake, Lake Shirley or Whalom
  22|             Zone II: The delineated recharge area to a public drinking water well as approved by MassDEP and defined
  23|             under the Massachusetts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00. For the purposes of this § 250-4.9,
  24|             Zone II shall not extend within that area included in Zone I. Accordingly, no parcels or portions of parcels lo-
  25|             cated within Zone I are considered to be included within Zone II.
  26|             Zone III: An area beyond a Zone II recharge area, as approved by MassDEP and defined under the Massachu-
  27|             setts Drinking Water Regulations 310 CMR 22.00.
  28|             D. ESTABLISHMENT AND DELINEATION OF WATER SUPPLY PROTECTION DISTRICT
  29|             For the purposes of this bylaw, there are hereby established within the Town of Lunenburg certain water sup-
  30|             ply protection areas a Zone I, Zone II, and Zone III which consist of a protective radius around a public water
  31|             supply well or wellfield (Zone I), aquifers or recharge areas (Zone II, and certain protected areas beyond those
  32|             Zone II areas (Zone III). These areas are delineated on a map entitled Lunenburg Water District Aquifer Pro-
  33|             tection Zones dated July 2021 which is hereby made part of the Water Supply Protection District Bylaw and is
  34|             on file in the office of the Town Clerk. Provided, however, Zone 1 ends at the water's edge and does not ex-
  35|             tend into the waters of Hickory Hills Lake, Lake Shirley or Whalom Lake.
  36|             E. DISTRICT BOUNDARY DISPUTES
  37|             1. If the location of the Water Supply Protection District in relation to a particular parcel is in doubt, resolu-
  38|                  tion of the boundary dispute shall be through a Special Permit application to the Special Permit Granting
  39|                   Authority. Any application for a special permit for this purpose shall be accompanied by adequate docu-
  40|                   mentation.
  41|                                                                                              168

===PAGE 169=== (ocr, 53 lines)
   1|             2. Burden of proof shall be upon the landowner to demonstrate that the location of the Water Supply Protec-
   2|                  tion District with respect to a particular parcel(s) of land is uncertain. At the request of the landowner, the
   3|                   Town may engage a professional engineer, hydrologist, geologist, or soil scientist to determine more accu-
   4|                  rately the boundaries of the Water Supply Protection District with respect to a particular parcel(s) of land,
   5|                  and may charge the owner for the cost of the investigation.
   6|                              A. Changes to the Water Supply Protection District require town meeting approval.
   7|                              B. Changes to the Zone II boundary require approval from the MassDEP
   8|             3. Where the Zone II boundary line of the Water Supply Protection District divides a lot or parcel, the re-
   9|                   quirements established by this bylaw shall apply to the land twenty (20) feet beyond the Zone II bounda-
  10|                  ry.
  11|             F. PERMITTED USES
  12|             1. Irrespective of the permitted uses in the underlying district, only the following uses are permitted within
  13|                   Zone I or Zone II of the Water Supply Protection District except as may also be allowed by Special Permit
  14|                  in § 250-4.9 H. This same restriction does not apply to Zone III.
  15|             2. The following uses are permitted within Zone I:
  16|                              A. Conservation of soil, water, plants, and wildlife;
  17|                              B. Outdoor recreation, nature study, swimming, boating, fishing, and hunting where otherwise le-
  18|                                    gally permitted;
  19|                              C. Raised wooden walkways, foot, bicycle and/or horse paths, and bridges;
  20|                              D. Normal operation and maintenance of existing water bodies and dams, splash boards, and other
  21|                                    water control, supply, and conservation devices;
  22|                              E. Uses accessory and subordinate to the above uses, if permitted in the underlying district and
  23|                                   subject to the provision of the underlying district, provided that the use does not result in cover-
  24|                                   age of more than 15% of the lot with impervious surfaces.
  25|                              F. Maintenance and repair of existing drinking water supply related works such as, but not limited
  26|                                   to, wells, pipelines, aqueducts, and tunnels.
  27|                              G. Removing non-native, nuisance, or invasive species, interplanting native species, and control-
  28|                                   ling species in a manner approved by Mass DEP that preserves drinking water quality and mini-
  29|                                   mizes damage to surrounding, non- target species.
  30|                              H. Maintaining vegetation as approved by MassDEP including pruning, trimming, cutting, mow-
  31|                                   ing, and removing brush to prevent, control, and manage hazards, disease, insect, or fire dam-
  32|                                   age, and to preserve drinking water quality and maintain the condition of the premises.
  33|             3. The following uses are permitted within Zone II and Zone III of the Water Supply Protection District, pro-
  34|                   vided that all necessary permits, orders, or approvals required by local, state, or federal law are also ob-
  35|                  tained:
  36|                              A. conservation of soil, water, plants, and wildlife;
  37|                              B. outdoor recreation, nature study, boating, fishing, and hunting where otherwise legally permit-
  38|                                   ted;
  39|                              C. foot, bicycle and/or horse paths, and bridges;
  40|                              D. normal operation and maintenance of existing water bodies and dams, splash boards, and other
  41|                                    water control, supply, and conservation devices;
  42|                              E. maintenance, repair, and enlargement of any existing structure, subject to § 250-4.9 G, § 250-
  43|                                   4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw;
  44|                              F. residential development, subject to § 250-4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning By-
  45|                                   law;
  46|                              G. farming, gardening, nursery, conservation, forestry, harvesting, and grazing, subject to § 250-
  47|                                   4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw;
  48|                              H. construction, maintenance, repair, and enlargement of existing drinking water supply related
  49|                                   facilities such as, but not limited to, wells, pipelines, aqueducts, and tunnels but shall not in-
  50|                                    clude construction of new treatment works and related;
  51|                              I. Water Remediation Treatment works approved by MassDEP designed for the treatment of con-
  52|                                   taminated ground or surface water operating in compliance with 314 CMR 5.05(3).
  53|                                                                                              169

===PAGE 170=== (ocr, 54 lines)
   1|                              J. any use permitted in the underlying zoning by-right or by special permit, except for those uses
   2|                              specifically prohibited in § 250-4.9 G, § 250-4.9 H and § 250-4.9 J of this Zoning Bylaw.
   3|             G. PROHIBITED USES
   4|             1. The following land uses and activities are prohibited in Zone II unless designed in accordance with the
   5|                  specified performance standards:
   6|                              A. landfills and open dumps;
   7|                              B. automobile graveyards and junkyards,;
   8|                              C. landfills receiving only wastewater residuals and/or septage, including those approved by
   9|                                    MassDEP pursuant to MGL c. 21 s.26 through s.53, MGL c.111 s.17, and MGL c.83 s.6 and
  10|                                   S.7;
  11|                              D. facilities that generate, treat, store, or dispose of hazardous waste that are subject to MGL c.21C
  12|                                   and 310 CMR 30.000, except for:
  13|                                    1. very small quantity generators as defined under 310 CMR 30.000;
  14|                                   2. household hazardous waste centers and events under 310 CMR 30.390;
  15|                                    3. waste oil retention facilities required by MGL c. 21, s.52A; and
  16|                                   4. water remediation treatment works approved by MassDEP for the treatment of contaminated
  17|                              waters subject to § 250- 4.9 H.
  18|                              E. petroleum, fuel oil, and heating oil bulk stations and terminals including, but not limited to
  19|                                   those listed under North American Industry Classification System (NAICS) Codes 424710 and
  20|                                   454311, except for liquefied petroleum gas.
  21|                              F. storage of liquid hazardous materials and/or liquid petroleum products unless such storage is
  22|                                    above ground level and on an impervious surface and either:
  23|                                    1. in container(s) or above ground tank(s) within a building; or
  24|                                    2.outdoors in covered container(s) or above ground tank(s) in an area that has a containment
  25|                                   system designed and operated to hold either; 10% of the total possible storage capacity of all
  26|                                   containers or 110% of the largest container's storage capacity, whichever is greater. However,
  27|                                   these storage requirements shall not apply to the replacement of existing tanks or systems for
  28|                                   the keeping, dispensing, or storing of gasoline provided the replacement is performed in a man
  29|                                   ner consistent with state and local requirements;
  30|                              G. storage of sludge and septage, unless such storage is in compliance with 310 CMR 32.30 and
  31|                                   310 CMR 32.31;
  32|                              H. storage of deicing chemicals, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and
  33|                                   ready for application, unless such storage, is within a structure designed to prevent the genera-
  34|                                   tion and escape of contaminated runoff or leachate;
  35|                                   storage of animal manure, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and ready
  36|                                   for application, unless contained within a structure designed to prevent the generation and es-
  37|                                   cape of contaminated runoff or leachate;
  38|                              J. storage of commercial fertilizers, with the exception of small quantities stored in containers and
  39|                                   ready for application, unless such storage is within a structure designed to prevent the genera-
  40|                                   tion and escape of contaminated runoff or leachate;
  41|                              K. stockpiling and disposal of snow and ice containing deicing chemicals brought in from outside
  42|                                   the Water Supply Protection District;
  43|                              L. earth removal, consisting of the removal of soil, loam, sand, gravel, or any other earth material
  44|                                   to within 4 feet of historical high groundwater as determined from monitoring wells and histori-
  45|                                   cal water table fluctuation data compiled by the United States Geological Survey, except for
  46|                                    excavations for building foundations, roads, utility works or wetland restoration work conduct-
  47|                                    ed in accordance with a valid Order of Condition issued pursuant to MGL c. 131, s.40; and
  48|                              M. treatment or disposal works subject to 314 CMR 5.00, for non- sanitary wastewater, including
  49|                                   those activities listed under 310 CMR 15.004(6), except for:
  50|                                   1. treatment works approved by MassDEP designed for the treatment of contaminated ground
  51|                                         or surface water and operating in compliance with 314 CMR 5.05(3) or 5.05(13) subject to
  52|                                         § 250-4.9 H; and
  53|                                   2. publicly owned treatment works subject to § 250-4.9 H.
  54|                                                                                              170

===PAGE 171=== (ocr, 48 lines)
   1|             H. USES AND ACTIVITIES REQUIRING A SPECIAL PERMIT
   2|             1. In Zone I the following uses and activities are permitted only upon the issuance of a Special Permit by the
   3|             Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) under such conditions as they may require:
   4|                              A. Construction of new treatment works and related structures.
   5|                              B. Water remediation treatment works except as expressly permitted in Section 250-4.9F(1)(i)
   6|                              C. Publicly owned treatment works.
   7|                              D. Construction of new water treatment works.
   8|             2. In Zone II, the following uses and activities are permitted only upon the issuance of a Special Permit by the
   9|             Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) under such conditions as they may require:
  10|                              A. enlargement or alteration of existing uses that do not conform to the Water Supply Protection
  11|                                    District;
  12|                              B. except as prohibited under § 250-4.9 G of this bylaw, activities that involve the handling of tox-
  13|                                   ic or hazardous materials in quantities greater than those associated with normal household use,
  14|                                   and which are permitted in the underlying zoning district;
  15|                              C. rendering impervious any lot or parcel more than 15% or 2,500 square feet, whichever is great-
  16|                                   er; unless artificial recharge, that will not degrade water quality, is provided using methods
  17|                                    demonstrated to be capable of removing contaminants from storm water and which are con-
  18|                                   sistent with methods described in MassDEP's Stormwater Handbook, Vol. I, II and III, as
  19|                                    amended.
  20|                              D. Construction of new treatment works and related structures.
  21|                              E. Water remediation treatment works except as expressly permitted in Section 250-4.9F(1)(i)
  22|                              F. Publicly owned treatment works.
  23|                              G. Construction of new water treatment works.
  24|             I. PROCEDURES FOR ISSUANCE OF SPECIAL PERMIT PURSUANT TO § 250-4.9
  25|             1. The Special Permit Granting Authority (SPGA) under this bylaw shall be the Planning Board. A special
  26|                  permit shall be granted if the SPGA determines, with advisement from the Lunenburg Water District,
  27|                   Building Commissioner, Conservation Commission, Department of Public Works, and Board of Health,
  28|                  that the intent of this bylaw, as well as its specific criteria, are met. The SPGA shall not grant a special per-
  29|                  mit under this section unless the petitioner's application materials include, in the SPA's opinion, suffi-
  30|                   ciently detailed, definite, and credible information to support positive findings in relation to the standards
  31|                   given in this section. The SPGA shall document the basis for any departures from the recommendations of
  32|                  the other municipal boards, departments, or commissions in its decision.
  33|             2. Upon receipt of the special permit application, the SPGA shall transmit one copy to the Lunenburg Water
  34|                   District, Building Commissioner, Conservation Commission, Department of Public Works, Board of
  35|                   Health, and Lunenburg Fire Department. Failure to respond in writing within 35 days of receipt shall be
  36|                   considered as no desire to comment. The necessary number of copies of the application shall be furnished
  37|                   by the applicant.
  38|             3. The SPGA may grant the required special permit upon finding that the proposed use meets the following
  39|                   standards, those standards and requirements specified in § 250-4.9 G of this bylaw, and any regulations or
  40|                   guidelines adopted by the SPGA. The proposed use must:
  41|                              A. in no way, during construction or thereafter, materially adversely affect the quality or quantity
  42|                                   of the water supplies protected by the Water Supply Protection District; and
  43|                              B. be designed to avoid substantial disturbance of the soils, topography, drainage, vegetation, and
  44|                                    other water-related natural characteristics of the site to be developed, except as depicted on an
  45|                                   approved site plan.
  46|             4. The SPGA may adopt controls to govern design features of projects. Such controls shall be consistent with
  47|             the Town's subdivision regulations, Site Plan Approval, and Stormwater Bylaws.
  48|                                                                                              171
